### 🔴 The procurement fact that actually moved: a policy destroyed by unverified AI output

🟢 **This is worth more to a studio engagement than any market number this pass returned**, and it
is why it is recorded here rather than in a policy tracker. `intel/market.md:2883` carries *"South
Africa — no national AI-in-education strategy, no regulator."* 🟢 **That absence now has a
mechanism:** the attempt reached public comment and was withdrawn in its entirety because its
citations were fabricated.

🔴 **What it changes about how AI work is bought in the public sector.** A ministry that has
publicly withdrawn a policy over hallucinated references will not accept an AI deliverable on the
strength of its fluency again. 🟢 **The billable consequence is provenance infrastructure:**
citation-level traceability, a verification record per claim, and a named human accountable for
each. 🔵 **This base is itself the argument** — every figure above carries its channel and its band,
and the one figure that could not be verified is labelled unverified rather than dropped. 🟢 **That
discipline is the deliverable, and this is the first engagement-grade evidence that a government
will pay for it.**

### 🔴 The procurement fact that actually moved: a licence, not a number

🟢 This base's flagship permissive agent — [`THU-MAIC/OpenMAIC`](https://github.com/THU-MAIC/OpenMAIC),
**41.3k★**, carried as `🟢 MIT` — ships **nine** grants. Eight are MIT (`1 065` B,
`sha256₁₂ ba2525637432`); the ninth, `packages/mathml2omml/LICENSE`, is **LGPL-3.0**
(`7 652` B, `sha256₁₂ e3a994d82e64`, manifest `"license": "LGPL-3.0-or-later"`).

🔴 **It is a direct root dependency** (`"mathml2omml": "workspace:*"`), built **first** in
`build:packages`, **35** paths, with **no root `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES`** — and it is what makes the
**PPTX / Office-math export** work, which is the capability the shelf recommends the project for.

🟢 **Why this belongs on the market shelf.** Under the EU AI Act an education deployment lands in
Annex III from **2027-12-02** and has to produce a **technical file**, and a technical file states
the licence of every component. 🔴 A proposal that prices *"OpenMAIC, MIT, PPTX export"* has
mis-stated a copyleft component in a document a conformity assessment will read. 🟢 **The remedy is
cheap if it is early**: keep the subtree unmodified and record it, or excise the PPTX path — and say
which, in the SoW, not after the audit.

### 🔴 This file's own `p411` row manufactured a refutation — the source was right, and the row proves it

🔴 [`intel/market.md:577`](../intel/market.md), written by **`p411`**, grades its APAC leg like this:

> *"🔴 **the leg re-surfaced a date this base has already REFUTED.** `p406` resolved exactly this
> contradiction: Decision `33/2026/QĐ-TTg` is in force **2026-08-15**, and pre-existing education
> systems have to **2027-09-01**, *not* 2027-03-01. 🔵 **A second independent sighting of the wrong
> Vietnamese date.**"*

🔴 **But the source it is grading is quoted on that same line, and it carries the qualifier:**
*"compliance deadlines in 1 March 2027, **plus a grace period for education systems already running
before 15 August 2026**."* 🟢 **That is this base's own settled structure**, and the row refutes
itself on its own line.

| instrument | verdict on the SAME two-tier structure |
|---|---|
| [`compose/patterns.md:1128`](../compose/patterns.md) | 🟢 *"**`2027-03-01`. That is the all-other-sectors date.** Education's is **`2027-09-01`** for a system live before **2026-08-15**, and **`2027-03-01`** only for one deployed inside the six months after Decision 33 took effect"* |
| `agents/trending.md:11066` (`p281`) | 🟢 *"Compliance `2027-03-01`; **+6 months** if the system was live before `2026-08-15`"* — graded **"correct"** in those words |
| `agents/trending.md:33591` | 🟢 education, health and banking to **`2027-09-01`**; *"all other existing high-risk systems until **2027-03-01**"* |
| 🔴 `intel/market.md:577` (`p411`) | 🔴 the identical structure graded **"the wrong Vietnamese date"** |

🔴 **And the mislabel inverts `p406`'s actual finding.** [`README.md:483`](../README.md) records what
`p406` caught: *"Vietnam's education deadline is **2027-09-01** … not the **2027-03-01** that
`intel/market.md`'s opportunity 5 and `Pattern C` both priced against. **Two of this base's three
green claims were wrong.**"* 🟢 `p406` caught **this base's own pricing rows**. 🔴 `p411` re-read that
as *external publishers keep printing a wrong date* — a category error that converts an
**internal-defect** finding into a **source-refutation** counter.

🟢 **This pass is the test case, because its own APAC leg returned the same structure**: *"a
compliance deadline of 1 March 2027 for the listed systems, with a six-month extension for education
and other sectors already operating before 15 August 2026."* 🔴 Graded by `p411`'s rule that is a
**third sighting of a wrong date**. 🟢 Graded by `compose/patterns.md:1128` it is **correct and
complete**. The second grading is the right one, and this pass records it as `CARRIED-CORRECT`.

#### 🟢 The discriminating test, and why the EMEA counter is NOT affected

🔵 A sighting counter over external sources is a good instrument — this base runs one, `ACTION SH`,
and it has *"caught seven times"* (`intel/market.md:1316`) the claim that EU high-risk duties land
**2026-08-02**. 🟢 **That counter is sound, and this pass fires it an eighth time**: one EMEA source
today states *"EU AI Act high-risk provisions enter into force Aug 2026"* **with no deferral
clause at all**.

🟢 **So the test is not which date the source leads with — it is whether the source carries the
qualifier.**

| leg today | what the source said | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| EMEA | *"high-risk provisions enter into force Aug 2026"* — **no** deferral mentioned | 🔴 **WRONG** — `ACTION SH` sighting **eight** |
| EMEA | Digital Omnibus moved Annex III stand-alone to **2027-12-02**, embedded to **2028-08-02** | 🟢 **CORRECT** — matches `Regulation (EU) 2026/1744` as carried |
| APAC | *"1 March 2027 … six-month extension for education … operating before 15 August 2026"* | 🟢 **CORRECT** — the qualifier is present; `p411`'s grading of this shape is withdrawn |

🟢 **`T97` (new): a refutation counter must quote the clause it says is missing.** `ACTION SH` can
name the absent deferral; `p411`'s Vietnam grading could not name an absent clause, because the
clause was in the quotation. 🔵 **Restated, not back-dated**, per `p352` / `p376`: `p411`'s row keeps
its text and gains a correction pointer to this section.

being counted as a wrong-date sighting. 🔵 Carried and unmoved: Korea's `AI Basic Act` in force
**2026-01-22** with MSIT running 2026 as a pilot year and a one-year penalty grace; Vietnam's
`Decision 33/2026/QĐ-TTg`, 46 systems / 6 sectors, education = 3; China's registration and labelling
regime; Japan's penalty-free `AI Promotion Act`; Singapore's agentic framework as guidance.
🔴 **Still unretrieved, and still a different statement from "policy-empty"** (`T45`): school-level
APAC rules.

#### 🟢 LATAM

🔴 **All carried** — UNESCO IESALC / UNU-IAS over **200** institutions in **19** countries, **87%**
using AI in at least one area against **26%** holding a formal strategy, **74%** in teaching and
learning; the UNESCO regional observatory launched **2026-04-14** at ECLAC Santiago; Microsoft
ConectAI toward **5 million** Brazilians by end-2027; Uruguay as the first regional signatory of the
Council of Europe AI convention; Peru's sandbox; Colombia's `CONPES 4144`; Brazil's ANPD pilot
sandbox running to **December 2026**. 🟢 **One genuinely new LATAM supply address, and it is not
buildable** — see `agents/top.md`: a Brazilian federal-institute grading agent that is
**ungranted**. 🔴 **An absence this pass DRAFTED and `gate-absence` refused before publication.** The LATAM leg's
own caveat read *"I found no binding, education-specific AI rules for schools in this search"*, and
this section was drafted carrying it. 🟢 **It is false against this base's own shelves, twice over:**
**Peru**'s Law **31814** + *reglamento* **DS 115-2025-PCM** activated **sector obligations naming
education on 2026-09-10** — a date that has already bitten — and **Colombia's `Ley 2626 de 2026`** is
recorded here as *"LATAM yields its first binding statute"*. 🔴 **Brazil still has none** (`PL
2338/2023` awaiting a Chamber vote after the October elections). 🟢 **So the finding is about the
CHANNEL, not the region:** a general-purpose LATAM leg does not retrieve the two binding instruments
this base already holds, and a pass that trusts its own leg's caveat will publish a false blank.
🔵 `intel/market.md:1710` records the same error class — *"the blank was false, and the correction was
already in the building"* — and `p277` **published** it where `gate-absence` stopped this pass.

## 🆕 `p415` · 2026-10-23 — **The agent tier re-measured FIRST-HAND against the live GitHub sidebars, and three figures this base carried are refuted — one of them by a factor of 31.** 🟢 Fifteen agent addresses and nine foundations read today, every licence taken from the repository's own sidebar. 🔴 AND the read channel matters: `curl` and `api.github.com` are REFUSED for every repository not attached to this session, so a pass that reports "unreachable" from `curl` alone is reporting a fact about the CHANNEL.

### 🔴 The market number is a RANGE, and the range is the finding

| Source | 2026 base | Forecast | CAGR |
|---|---|---|---|
| IMARC Group | **~$9.0B** | $79.6B by **2034** | 31.35% |
| The Business Research Company | **~$10.6B** | $42.48B by **2030** | 41.5% |
| Technavio | *(no base published)* | **+$3.37B incremental**, 2025–2030 | 45% |
| Azumo (compilation) | North America **$3.68B** (2026) | $136.79B by 2035 | 34.52% |

🔴 **The 2026 base ranges from ~$3B to ~$10.6B across these four — a 3.5× spread on the same year.**
They are vendor research estimates measuring different scopes (tools only vs. tools + services vs.
institutional spend). 🔵 **How to use this in a proposal:** cite the spread and the scope question,
never a single headline figure. A client who has read two of these reports already knows they
disagree, and a deck that picks the biggest number tells them which one you read.

### 🟢 The adoption numbers that are worth more than the market size

- **RAND**, 2025: **54% of students** and **53% of teachers** used AI for school work. 🟢 The most
  defensible adoption figure found across every leg this pass — a named research institution with a
  published method.
- 🔴 **Treat with suspicion:** "83% of K-12 teachers", "student AI usage from 66% to 92%". Both come
  from vendor compilations, both are attributed to surveys the compilation does not link, and the
  second is a 26-point jump in a single year. **Do not put either in a client deck.**
- **HolonIQ**, 2026 snapshot: buyers increasingly demand *evidence* that a product improves learning
  quality, persistence, well-being or job-relevant skills. 🟢 This is the procurement reality that
  actually governs an engagement, and it is a stronger argument than any market-size slide.

### 🟢 The players, by layer

- **Platform incumbents:** Moodle, Open edX / 2U, Instructure (Canvas), D2L (Brightspace), Anthology.
  All copyleft or proprietary; none of them is where a studio adds value.
- **Open model suppliers relevant to education:** OpenEuroLLM consortium (EMEA), Teuken-7B / OpenGPT-X
  (Germany), Apertus (Switzerland), plus open-weight general models served locally.
- **Open agent supply:** one hub (DeepTutor, Apache-2.0, 42.3k★) and a long tail — see
  [`agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md) for the measured distribution.
- **Public-sector stacks:** Sunbird (India, MIT), Kolibri (MIT, global south deployments).

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🆕 `p420` 🟢 **The California bill on this row now has a number: `AB 1159`** — it bars student data from being used to train AI models. 🟢 **And a denominator for the activity:** **134** AI-in-education bills across **31** states (MultiState tracker, **2026-04**), with **Oklahoma** and **Maryland** requiring human oversight and barring AI from high-stakes decisions about students. 🆕 **Canada: the *"AI for All"* strategy (2026-06)** carries national AI-literacy work with **no legislative date set** — a guidance market, not a compliance one. 🆕 **Mexico: bills pending, no regulator to enforce them.** 🟡 Band `reported`: NA holds **36 %** of the AI-in-education market and **60 %** of US K-12 teachers used AI in 2024-25 (**32 %** weekly) — aggregator figures, not primary.

🆕 `p418` 🟡 **One secondary addition, not priced.** **Maryland's Artificial Intelligence Ready Schools Act** requires all **24** districts to adopt AI policies, with state guidance across eight areas — the district-mandate shape, which is a procurement trigger rather than a market size. 🔵 Single-source and **0 hits** on these shelves before today. 🟢 **The opportunity is unchanged in kind:** a state that mandates a policy per district creates demand for *policy-to-control* work — mapping a written AI policy onto the human-oversight and logging surface a deployment actually has. 🔴 And from this pass: **a component licence belongs in that mapping** — a district policy that says "open source only" is not satisfied by a repository whose root says MIT and whose export path is LGPL-3.0.

🟢 **Policy, measured.** 35 states plus Puerto Rico now publish official guidance on AI use in K-12;
binding statute is far rarer than guidance. Named instruments this pass: **Idaho SB 1227** (statewide
K-12 AI framework; prohibits AI *replacing* a human teacher), **Arizona HB 4040** (K-12 *and* public
universities must adopt student-AI-use policies), **Maryland** (designated AI coordinators;
university-supported certification of compliant tools), **Florida** (a rule reaching kindergarten
through graduate level), and a **California** bill barring student data from training AI models
unless the school directly benefits. Federally, the **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026
(H.R. 8747)** was advanced by a House committee on **2026-07-21**; if enacted it lets schools spend
federal funds on AI literacy.

🟢 **The opportunity.** Not the tutor — the **compliance envelope around the tutor**. Every instrument
above asks an institution to prove *which tool, approved by whom, doing what to which data, with a
human where*. Build: Canvas-side agents on [`canvas-mcp`](https://github.com/vishalsachdev/canvas-mcp)
(MIT), learner state in [`tutor-mcp`](https://github.com/ArnaudGuiovanna/tutor-mcp) (MIT), BKT
mastery from [`OATutor`](https://github.com/CAHLR/OATutor) (MIT, UC Berkeley — a provenance that
survives a procurement review), and ship the audit log as a **deliverable, not a feature**.
🔴 **Do not propose anything that reads as replacing a teacher** — in Idaho it is prohibited by name,
and elsewhere it is the fastest way to lose a board vote.

🔵 **`p416` adds the readiness denominator, and it sharpens this pitch rather than changing it.** A
2026 statistics round-up puts North America at a **36%** regional share (a second vendor source says
**37.5%** — 🔴 they disagree, use neither as a point estimate) while only **10% of institutions
publish formal AI guidelines** and **71% of US teachers report no AI training**. 🔴 Single vendor
round-up, secondary, uncorroborated — a hypothesis to test in discovery, not a figure for a deck.
🟢 Read against the 35-states-plus-Puerto-Rico guidance count above, the implication is that the
binding constraint is **not policy but institutional capacity to comply with it**: the compliance
envelope is the product, and teacher enablement is the line item most buyers have not yet budgeted.

### EMEA

🆕 `p420` 🔴 **The obligation on this row is a RUNWAY, not a live duty, and the date is the deliverable.** The AI omnibus entered into force **2026-07-27** and AI Act enforcement began **2026-08-02** — but **education is Annex III high-risk and its obligations moved to 2027-12-02**, with **2028-08-02** for AI embedded in regulated products. 🟢 **Live now regardless:** staff AI-literacy (softened to *support*), transparency, deepfake disclosure. 🔵 Schools are **deployers**; the Act reaches **non-EU EdTech** placing product on the EU market, and the **Apply AI Alliance** is the Commission's industry forum. 🆕 **Middle East: the UAE made AI a mandatory school subject from the 2025-26 year.** 🟡 The deferral is band `reported` — two education-sector sources agree, one dissenter still prints 2026-08-02 and is discounted; the amendment text was not read.

🆕 `p419` 🟢 **Africa re-enters this bucket with a procurement lesson rather than a market number, and `p418`'s nil is withdrawn.** South Africa's draft National AI Policy (DCDT, **2026-04-10**, comment to **2026-06-10**) was **withdrawn 2026-04-26** by Minister Solly Malatsi and **gazetted 2026-06-12** in its entirety, because **at least 10 % of its academic references were fictitious**; a replacement is targeted for FY **2026/27** and none is published. 🟡 Band `reported` — `sanews.gov.za` plus DLA Piper, ITWeb and CNBC Africa; the gazette text is unreachable from this environment's egress allowlist. 🟢 **The opportunity is provenance infrastructure, and it is now evidenced rather than argued:** a ministry that withdrew a policy over hallucinated citations will not buy an AI deliverable on fluency again, so citation-level traceability, a per-claim verification record and a named accountable human are **billable conformity work** — and they are the same artefacts Annex III's technical file will demand from **2027-12-02**. 🔵 Region is **EMEA** per this file's closed vocabulary; the country belongs in the prose, which is where it is. 🔴 **Still no binding AI statute anywhere in Africa or the Middle East** — strategies only (Saudi Arabia, the UAE, Egypt, Nigeria, Kenya, Rwanda, Morocco, South Africa).

🆕 `p418` 🔴 **Nil new, and the leg is now worth less than the re-read it displaces.** The EMEA leg returned the AI Act dates, the Digital Omnibus (`Regulation (EU) 2026/1744`) and the Commission guidelines — **all carried, all at this base's corrected values** (Annex III education **`2027-12-02`**, Art. 50 live **`2026-08-02`**, embedded **`2028-08-02`**). 🟢 **The source even flagged its own timing conflict**, so `ACTION SH` did **not** fire. 🔴 **Africa and the Middle East returned nothing again.** 🟢 **The live EMEA opportunity sharpened by this pass is the technical file**: Annex III bites 2027-12-02, a technical file names every component's licence, and this pass found a copyleft subtree inside a repository the market calls MIT. **Component-licence attestation is billable conformity work, and it is work that must happen before the build, not after.**

🟢 **Policy, measured — and the timing changed.** Secondary sources consistently report that
**Regulation (EU) 2026/1744 moved the AI Act's stand-alone high-risk education deadline from
2026-08-02 to 2027-12-02**. 🔴 **Not verified against the Official Journal this pass — verify before
it reaches a client document.** What is *already* live is not deferred: **Article 50 transparency**
applies from **2026-08-02** (a student-facing chatbot must disclose that it is AI), and **emotion
recognition in education institutions has been prohibited since February 2025**, subject to the
medical/safety exception. Annex III education uses are admission, assessing learning outcomes where
that steers the learning process, and monitoring behaviour during tests. The **Schola Europaea**
guidelines go further than the Act for European Schools: pupils **cannot be required** to use genAI,
and tools must be approved first.

🟢 **Sovereignty is the other half.** **OpenEuroLLM** (~20 partners, ~€20M of a €37.4M budget from
the Digital Europe Programme; open weights, open data, EU-resident training and inference),
**Teuken-7B** (OpenGPT-X), **Apertus**, and **SOOFI** (Fraunhofer/DFKI, 100B, first public version
targeted Q3 2026 per a secondary source). 🔴 **Licence trap:** Aleph Alpha's public Pharia releases
are research/education non-commercial — read the terms before a school deployment.
On the programme side, **AI-ENTR4YOUTH** (JA Europe, with Intel and the European Commission) piloted
in Italy, Portugal and Spain and has expanded to Albania, Bulgaria, Czechia, France, Greece, Romania
and Ukraine; teacher training is reported as the binding constraint on scale.

🟢 **The opportunity.** The only permissive full LMS on these shelves is **European**:
[`OpenOLAT`](https://github.com/OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT) (Apache-2.0). Pair it with EU-resident
open-weight inference, Article 50 disclosure built into the UI rather than bolted on, and a
documented *absence* of emotion recognition. 🔵 **Sell the conformity dossier as part of the build** —
in EMEA it is the deliverable that closes the deal, and the deferral to 2027-12-02 is exactly the
window in which a client can get ahead of it rather than scramble.

🔵 **`p416` adds the dispersion, and it is the reason a single EMEA go-to-market is wrong.** Eurostat
2025 figures cited by the EESC put enterprise AI adoption between **5.21% (Romania)** and
**42.03% (Denmark)** — an eight-fold spread inside one regulatory regime — with the top barriers for
firms that considered AI and did not adopt being lack of expertise (**70.89%**), uncertainty about
legal consequences (**52.52%**) and data-protection concern (**48.83%**). 🟡 EU-institution note
citing Eurostat: the strongest secondary source on this file's regional figures, still secondary.
🟢 **The AI Act makes the obligations uniform; it does not make the readiness uniform.** Price,
stage and staff an EMEA engagement off the member state, not off the region. 🔵 `p416` re-ran the
EMEA regulatory leg and it returned the **2027-12-02** Annex III deferral independently —
corroboration of a figure this base reached first, and the Official-Journal check flagged above
remains outstanding, because two secondary sources do not make a primary one.

### APAC

🆕 `p420` 🟢 **The Vietnamese date on this row is CONFIRMED from a second, independent source:** Law on AI **No. 134/2025/QH15**, in force **2026-03-01**, with **education, health and finance extended to 2027-09-01**. 🟢 **South Korea's AI Basic Act has been in force since 2026-01-22.** 🆕 **And the politics of classroom AI are contested across the whole region:** Ipsos Education Monitor 2026 support for *banning* AI in schools runs Indonesia **23 %** · Thailand **25 %** · India **26 %** · Singapore **28 %** · Japan **29 %** · Malaysia **29 %** · South Korea **31 %**, with ANZ higher — an ~8-point spread across seven systems. 🔵 **Australia:** National AI Plan (2025-12) leans on existing technology-neutral law, **mandatory guardrails paused**. 🔴 `T45` holds: **school-level** rules remain the informed gap.

🆕 `p418` 🟢 **Third independent corroboration of the Vietnam two-tier structure, and the grace period is explicit this time.** The leg returned *"comply by 1 March 2027, except for those in healthcare, education and finance already in operation before 15 August 2026, in which case an additional six-month grace period applies"* — i.e. this base's **`2027-09-01`** for pre-existing education systems, stated as a duration rather than a date. 🔵 **Korea's instrument was served as the "AI Basic Act"** where these shelves carry *"Framework Act"* — **naming variance on one instrument**, same in-force date **`2026-01-22`**; recorded so a successor does not shelve it twice. 🟢 **The opportunity:** an installed-base deployment in Vietnam has until `2027-09-01` and a greenfield one until `2027-03-01` — **six months of arbitrage that favours retrofitting an existing system over replacing it**, which is the opposite of the usual modernisation pitch. 🔴 **School-level rules remain the informed gap** (`T45`).

🟢 **Policy, measured.** Four different regimes, not one: **South Korea** has a general AI law in
force; **China** has no general AI law but binding sectoral rules, including **mandatory labelling of
AI-generated content since 2025-09-01**; **Japan**'s AI Promotion Act carries **no fines or
prohibitions** (a second AI Basic Plan is reported as approved 2026-07-14 — 🔴 flagged unverified by
the source itself); **India** has no AI-specific law, applies its data-protection law, and requires
**prominent labels and provenance markers on AI-generated content from 2026-02-20**.

🟢 **Curriculum mandates are the demand driver.** China has made AI teaching compulsory from **age
six**; India plans AI and computational thinking **compulsory from Class 3 in AY 2026-27**. Ipsos
Education Monitor 2026 finds *lower* support for banning AI in schools across the Asian markets
surveyed, with Australia and New Zealand notably *more* pro-ban — 🔵 a real split inside the region
that a single APAC go-to-market will get wrong.

🟢 **The opportunity, and it is a supply advantage.** **Most of the verified permissive education
agent supply on these shelves is APAC-origin**: DeepTutor (Apache-2.0, HKU), Study-Mate (MIT),
Bloom (MIT), pyKT (MIT), educhain (MIT, India), Sunbird (MIT, India). An APAC engagement can compose
almost entirely from in-region, permissively-licensed code. Build content-provenance labelling in
from the first sprint — it is required in both China and India, and it is far cheaper as a design
constraint than as a retrofit.

🔵 **`p416`: nothing unshelved on the APAC leg.** It returned vendor sizing (**~$592M in 2024 →
~$1.85B by 2029, 20.9% CAGR**) and the fragmentation point this block already carries. 🟢 The supply
advantage above was re-confirmed first-hand today instead: `ai-shifu` (**Apache-2.0, 326★**, Research
Center of Intelligent Software Engineering, Harbin Institute of Technology) and `pykt-toolkit`
(**MIT, 451★**) both read clean, as did `pyedmine` (MIT, 82★) and `EduStudio` (MIT, 79★). 🔴 Note the
concentration while you sell it: the whole knowledge-tracing research tier on these shelves is
China-origin, and a client will find that in the dependency tree whether or not you say it first.

### LATAM

🆕 `p420` 🔴 **The adoption-governance gap on this row is now a measured pair, not a slogan:** UNESCO IESALC across **200 institutions in 19 countries** finds **87 % already using AI** and **only 26 % with a formal AI strategy**. 🟢 **Teachers are ahead of the OECD mean** — TALIS 2024: Brazil **56 %**, Chile **55 %**, Colombia **53 %**, Costa Rica **52 %** against an OECD average of **36 %**; Uruguay's Ceibal reports **75 %** of public-school teachers. 🔴 **Students are the gap: 1 in 2 report confusion about appropriate use** — so the first deliverable is a policy and a student-facing literacy track, not a model. 🆕 **UNESCO launched the Observatory on AI in Education for LAC on 2026-04-14 in Santiago.** 🟢 **And this pass shelved the first LATAM-placed permissive grading asset:** `dsvi-b/corretor-enem` (MIT, Brazil, 11 147 calibrated ENEM essays) — see `Pattern S`.

🆕 `p418` 🟡 **One secondary addition: Muyu Education (Ecuador)**, applying AI to **teacher development** — **0 hits** here before today, from HolonIQ's 2026 LATAM EdTech 100. 🟢 **The governance gap is re-confirmed and it is the regional thesis:** UNESCO IESALC finds **87 %** of 200 HEIs across 19 countries using AI in at least one area against only **26 %** with a formal AI strategy, and adoption is highest at private non-profit universities while public institutions are resource-constrained. 🟢 **That 87/26 split is a demand signal for governance delivery, not for more tooling** — the institutions have the tools and lack the policy, oversight and evidence layer. 🔵 Brazil's **56 %** of secondary teachers against the OECD's **36 %** says the same at school level. 🔴 **And LATAM is where this pass's licence finding lands hardest**: public-sector procurement here leans on "open source" as a *category*, and a category check does not catch a copyleft subtree under an MIT root.

🟢 **Policy, measured.** **Mexico closed its legislative period with no general AI law**; the pattern
is sectoral patches. The one education-specific instrument found: the **Estado de México reformed
article 61 of its state education law in April 2026** to promote responsible AI use in upper-secondary
and higher education. **Brazil's PL 2338/2023 has been approved by the Senate** — 🔴 status in the
Câmara dos Deputados **not confirmed this pass**.

🔴 **Adoption figures diverge by an order of magnitude because they measure different things.**
EFE, August 2026: **Brazil ~76%, Mexico ~70%** — of *digital users*. Enterprise adoption, Q1 2026:
**Brazil 41%, Chile 34%, Argentina 29%, Colombia 24.5%, Mexico 20.1%**. 🔵 Both are real; quoting the
first as institutional readiness is the error, and it is a very easy one to make in a deck.

🔴 **The supply gap, stated plainly and for the fourth pass running.** There is **no mature
LATAM-origin open-source education agent**. Two Spanish-language legs this pass returned exactly one
on-brief address — [`papayaykware/agente-educativo-psicocognitivo`](https://github.com/papayaykware/agente-educativo-psicocognitivo)
(MIT, **0 stars**, roadmap-stage, DKT + affect detection). It is a reference design, not a dependency.

🟢 **The opportunity follows from the gap, not despite it.** LATAM engagements compose
**global permissive components with regional delivery constraints**:
[`Kolibri`](https://github.com/learningequality/kolibri) (MIT, offline-first) for connectivity,
[`educhain`](https://github.com/satvik314/educhain) (MIT) for Spanish/Portuguese item generation,
[`fsrs-rs`](https://github.com/open-spaced-repetition/fsrs-rs) (BSD-3) for review scheduling, local
open-weight inference for cost and data residency. 🔵 And the gap itself is the pitch: **being the
team that publishes the first credible Spanish/Portuguese education agent is available**, and it
costs a fraction of what the equivalent position in NA or APAC would.

🔵 **`p416` reframes the gap: it is not "none was built", it is "the one that was built is frozen".**
[`menthorlabs/menthor`](https://github.com/menthorlabs/menthor) — **Apache-2.0, 582★, Portuguese
README**, a programming-teaching platform — was re-read first-hand today and is **archived by its
owner since `2024-12-12`, read-only**. 🔴 So the fifth consecutive nil on the Spanish/Portuguese legs
sits next to a real, permissively-licensed, 582-star LATAM artefact that nobody maintains. 🟢 That
changes the pitch from *build the first* to **the first already exists and is unowned**: an
Apache-2.0 fork with a public maintenance commitment is cheaper, faster and more defensible as a
regional position than a greenfield agent, and it inherits the stars, the history and the Portuguese
content rather than starting at zero the way `agente-educativo-psicocognitivo` does.

### Global

🆕 `p420` 🔴 **A licence-channel risk that applies to every region, and it is not about permissiveness.** Measured first-hand this pass: a **root-path licence read misgrades 13 of 19** addresses on these shelves. Moodle grants at root `COPYING.txt` on `main` but at `lang/en/docs/licence.html` on its `_STABLE` LTS refs; the Moodle **plugin** ecosystem grants only in `version.php` headers; Frappe's three platforms grant at lowercase `license.txt` (ERPNext **GPL-3.0**, LMS **AGPL-3.0-or-later**). 🟢 **The sector's grants are overwhelmingly COPYLEFT**, and `Gap 298` is confirmed on a **seventh** channel: no major education LMS or SIS is permissive except the already-shelved Apache-2.0 **OpenOLAT**. 🟡 **The studio risk is pricing a copyleft component as permissive**, not failing to find a licence.

🆕 `p419` 🔴 **A diligence step this base can now name, because it cost a wrong answer this pass: a repository's grant is resolved per `(ref, path)`, not per repository.** [`piyushdhoka/GradeU`](https://github.com/piyushdhoka/GradeU) ships three applications on three branches — student site on `main` (🟢 **MIT**), teacher studio on `teacher` and admin portal on `admin` (🔴 **both UNGRANTED**: no licence-shaped path at any depth, no manifest `license` field) — and its badge, its sidebar and any default-branch licence read all say MIT. 🔴 **If the component an engagement needs is the author-side or tenant-admin app, the MIT grant covers none of the code that would ship.** 🟢 **The check is one command before the path enumeration — `git ls-remote --heads`, then enumerate per ref** — and it needs no API, no HTML and no new channel. 🔴 **It cannot be done on `raw.githubusercontent.com`**, which serves `master` and `HEAD` as aliases for the default branch even when no such ref exists (measured on three repositories, with `trunk`/`develop`/bogus-ref negative controls at `404`), so a `200` there proves a file and says nothing about a branch. 🟢 **Priced at step 2 of a licence audit, this is minutes; discovered at a client's legal review, it is a re-platform.**

🆕 `p418` 🟢 **One rule to add, from this pass's only movement.** A repository's licence is a **set, not a value** — enumerate every licence-shaped path before a single cell is written, because a workspace monorepo makes a vendored third-party package (`workspace:*`) look like first-party code. 🔵 Measured on `OpenMAIC`: **9** grants, **8 × MIT** and **1 × LGPL-3.0-or-later**, the copyleft one under the export path the project is recommended for. 🟢 **The check costs one `git clone --filter=blob:none` and one `ls-tree | grep`** — put it in the diligence step of every engagement, in every region, because it is the cheapest finding on this page and the most expensive one to discover late.

🟢 **What holds everywhere.** Buyers have moved from "can it generate" to "can you evidence it"
(HolonIQ) and from tool sprawl to governance (1EdTech). The composition rule is constant across all
four regions: **permissive agent layer, copyleft platform underneath, MCP as the boundary between
them.** That single architectural choice is what makes one codebase sellable in all four regulatory
regimes — Article 50 disclosure in EMEA, provenance labelling in APAC, audit logs in North America
and offline operation in LATAM are then four configurations of one system, not four products.

🔵 **`p416`: the composition rule gains a SECOND boundary.** MCP is the boundary between the agent
layer and the platform. **The package format is the boundary between the content layer and the
platform** — and as of today these shelves hold a readable permissive emitter for it,
[`DozenTwelve/Papermorph`](https://github.com/DozenTwelve/Papermorph) (**MIT**, 849★, SCORM 1.2 /
2004). 🟢 An artefact that leaves the build as a SCORM package installs through a door the client's
LMS already has open: Moodle takes it as a core activity, Canvas and OpenOLAT import it. 🟡 Open edX
needs a community `scormxblock` first, and Kolibri's pipeline is its own — 🟡 the ingest side was not
re-read first-hand this pass, so confirm against the client's actual version before it reaches a
statement of work. 🔴 And state the ceiling in the first conversation, not the third: **SCORM carries
completion, score and time and nothing about mastery, affect or trajectory.**

## 🆕 `p414` · 2026-10-23 — **`34 501` is NOT the AGPL-3.0 `http` form — it is ONE repository's de-indented title line, and the spread is a 2×2 lattice over `34 520` whose four cells are all now measured. 🔴 `education-agent-skills`' grant is a POINTER on BOTH its subtrees and its operative text is unreachable here. 🔴 AND the egress refusal has TWO classes these shelves record as one.**

🔵 **Dating note.** Every search leg, licence read, registry probe, channel probe and gate run below
was executed on **2026-10-23**, and the commit lands **2026-10-23**. 🔵 **This is the twentieth pass
dated today**, after `p395`–`p413`.

🟢 **Gates FIRST, per the standing rule `p412` set and `p413` kept.** `make hooks` ran **before** any
research: **50 of 51 gates green on the FIRST run** — equal to `p413`'s best-ever first-run state and
again against `47` for both `p409` and `p412`. 🔵 The lone red is `gate-census-union` control 24,
**`Gap 400-B`**, carried deliberately red since `p398`; `p387-G` forbids closing it by weakening a
control and this pass did not try. 🟢 **`p413` left the base clean** — the currency gates were green
on arrival for the second consecutive pass.

🔵 **Channel note, and it CORRECTS `p413`'s largest finding rather than repeating it.** `p413`
concluded the egress channel is an **allowlist** and recorded its evidence as *"`http=000` —
connection never established, **not a `403`**, not a `404`"*. 🔴 **There is a `403`, and it is at the
`CONNECT` layer**: `curl` prints `CONNECT tunnel failed, response 403` and the proxy names
`connect_rejected` — *"the host is not allowed for this session"*. 🟢 **`p413`'s conclusion was right
and its evidence was under-read**, and reading it properly splits what these shelves record as one
refusal into **two classes with different remedies**:

| class | http | body | what it means | remedy here |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **SERVED** | `200` | yes | `pypi.org`, `raw.githubusercontent.com`, `registry.npmjs.org` | — |
| 🟢 **SEMANTIC-404** | `404` | yes (21 B) | the registry **answered**; the absence is a fact | — |
| 🔴 **AUTHZ-REFUSED** | `403` | **yes, 378 B** | tunnel opens; the body names the **session's repository scope** (`api.github.com`, `codeload.github.com`) | a scope grant — **not** a host problem |
| 🔴 **TUNNEL-REFUSED** | `000` | **no** | `CONNECT` 403 + `connect_rejected`; host **not on the session allowlist** | 🔴 **none on this environment** |

🔵 Fifteen URLs, control pair first, in
[`evidence/refusal-taxonomy.2026-10-23-p414.tsv`](../evidence/refusal-taxonomy.2026-10-23-p414.tsv);
the probe is [`compose/code/p414-refusal-taxonomy/refusal.sh`](../compose/code/p414-refusal-taxonomy/refusal.sh).
🟢 **Why the split pays:** an `AUTHZ-REFUSED` host is reachable and merely unauthorised, so a figure
behind it is *obtainable in principle*; a `TUNNEL-REFUSED` host is not, so a figure behind it is
**unverifiable on this channel, permanently**, and `p413`'s standing register rule applies. 🔴 This
base's own [`p798-proxy-refusal-ledger`](../compose/code/p798-proxy-refusal-ledger/README.md) records
refusals **without** this class split — successor item 1.

### 🟢 What this pass found

| # | finding |
|---|---|
| 1 | 🔴 **`make hooks` runs, and the two passes that said otherwise left a gate RED behind them.** 47/51 green. The four reds: `gate-author-key`, `gate-census-currency`, `gate-census-union`, `gate-union-currency`. 🔴 **`gate-author-key` was broken by `p411` itself** — the row it wrote at `repos/trending.md` published *“123 commits, 5 authors”* naming **neither `%ae` nor `%an`**, which is precisely what `ACTION ZZ-ac` exists to forbid. 🔵 A pass that declines to run the gates cannot see the gate it breaks; that is the whole mechanism, and it is now measured rather than argued |
| 2 | 🔴 **“5 authors” is the `%an` count. By `%ae` it is 3 — and `p411` published the list that refutes it.** `Gego-K12/gegok12`, full history re-derived this pass: **123 commits, 3 distinct `%ae`, 5 distinct `%an`**. `karthick@gegosoft.com` ships under **three** display names (`Karthick` 34, `GegoSoft` 4, `karthick` 1 = **39**, exact). 🔴 `devteam@gegok12.com` is a **shared ROLE account carrying 83 of 123 commits (67.5 %)**. 🔵 **No new probe was needed:** `repos/foundations.md` already carried all five names *and their counts*, so the collapse was derivable from this base's own published cell |
| 3 | 🔴 **`Gap 298` does NOT close on authorship, and the `p410`→`p411` narrowing is reversed.** `p410` moved the absence to *multi-author* permissive SIS code; `p411` declared that *“answered too — a company, five authors, a container.”* 🔴 On the keyed figure it is **one role account plus one human with more than a single commit**. 🔵 The MIT grant, the 7 623 paths, the container chain and the company are all real and unaffected — **what fails is the authorship inference alone**, and with it `Pattern AH`'s second justification |
| 4 | 🟢 **The GPL-3.0 “denominator spread” is NOT noise, needs NO authoritative remote channel, and resolves to a two-value lookup.** `p411` measured 35 141 / 35 147 / 35 149 B and concluded that with `gnu.org` unreachable *“no authoritative denominator exists on this channel at all.”* 🟢 **Measured in-tree this pass: `moodle/moodle` `COPYING.txt` = 35 147 B and `frappe/erpnext` `license.txt` = 35 149 B differ on EXACTLY FOUR LINES, every one a URL-scheme modernisation**, and the arithmetic closes exactly: three lines gain **+1 B** (`http:`→`https:`), the fourth gains +1 and loses 2 (`philosophy/why-not-lgpl.html` → `licenses/why-not-lgpl.html`) ⇒ **35 147 + 2 = 35 149** ✔. Root `LICENSE` is **874 B**, every line prefixed `#`, titled *“Apache License / Version 2.0”* and carrying `SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0`. 🔴 **Apache-2.0's canonical payload is 11,358 B, fetched and byte-counted this pass — so this file is 7.7 % of the licence it names.** It is the ~175-word per-file appendix, pasted at the grant path. 🔴 **And `composer.json` says `"license": "MIT"`** — two in-tree channels, two different answers, while the search result said Apache-2.0 |
| 2 | 🔴 **The byte-size tell cuts both ways, and here it cuts the wrong way.** 874 B sits **inside the MIT band** this pass measured first-hand (1,066–1,110 B over four repositories). 🔴 So a size-only classifier mis-sorts this file toward **MIT** — which is exactly what the manifest claims, so **size and manifest would agree on the wrong answer** while the title line and the SPDX id both say Apache-2.0. 🔵 Only the *ratio against the named licence* separates them |
| 3 | 🔴 **A dual grant whose copyleft half is a 302-byte stub.** [`ivan-my-wong/flowclass`](https://github.com/ivan-my-wong/flowclass) — `main`, HEAD `bdbc79b` **2026-09-20 · 33 d**. Its README §Licenses: *“Root open-source code: MIT (`LICENSE`)”* / *“Self-host/server copyleft terms: AGPL-3.0 (`LICENSE-AGPL`)”*. 🟢 `LICENSE` is a real MIT payload, **1,066 B**. 🔴 **`LICENSE-AGPL` is 302 B — 0.9 % of an AGPL payload** — and its text delegates: *“For the full legal text, see: https://www.gnu.org/licenses/agpl-3.0.txt”*. 🔴 **`package.json` says `"license": "SEE LICENSE IN LICENSE"`, pointing at the MIT half only** — the manifest under-reports the obligation in the dangerous direction |
| 4 | 🔴 **An instrument finding, on this base's own canonical vocabulary.** [`p176-grant-path-vocabulary`](../compose/code/p176-grant-path-vocabulary/grant_paths.py) exists so *“an absence claim names the vocabulary it was reached with”* — and its `SPLIT_FAMILY` is **permissive-only**: `("LICENSE-MIT", "LICENSE-APACHE")`. **`is_grant_path("LICENSE-AGPL")` is `False`.** 🔵 The shape *is* known to this base — 7 files under `compose/code/` carry it — but **every one is a shell fetcher (`p211`, `p213`) or a result TSV (`p191`, `p444`, `p459`), never the vocabulary**, and the two modules that import it (`p253`, `p329`) inherit the omission. 🔴 **`flowclass` is the live instance:** probe it with v1, stop at the first hit (`AMERICAN` is ordered first *by design*, so the probe stops early), and record **MIT** — never learning the project calls itself AGPL-3.0 when self-hosted. 🔴 The omission runs **one way**: `LICENSE-GPL`, `LICENSE-AGPL`, `LICENSE-COMMERCIAL` are all the *restricting* half. This is the 76-list fragmentation `p176` was built to end, still unresolved in the copyleft direction |

## 🆕 `p406` · 2026-10-23 — the APAC leg RESOLVED this file's own Vietnam contradiction, and it refutes a live opportunity row

🔴 **For a THIRD consecutive pass the four regional legs returned almost no unshelved fact — and the
exception is the one that matters most, because this file has been contradicting ITSELF about
Vietnam's education deadline since `p388` and a published opportunity is keyed on the wrong date.**

### 🔴 The contradiction, as this file carried it into today

| where | what it said |
|---|---|
| line ~57 *(green)* | Vietnam's AI law *"education high-risk, compliance to **2027-09**"* |
| line ~1733 *(green)* | systems live *"before **15 August 2026** get a **six-month** grace period"* |
| opportunity 5 *(green, a LIVE recommendation)* | *"a dated Vietnam roadmap … compliance **1 Mar 2027**"* |
| `p388` *(amber, deliberately not promoted)* | *"sources disagree on Vietnam's dates … read the instrument before quoting any Vietnamese date"* |

🟢 **They do not disagree. There are TWO obligations and this file folded them into one.** Measured
across five independent law-firm and practice readings that **concur row for row**:

| instrument | |
|---|---|
| **Law No. 134/2025/QH15** *(Law on Artificial Intelligence)* | in force **2026-03-01** |
| **Decree 142/2026/ND-CP** | implementing |
| **Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg** | issued **2026-06-30**, lists **46 high-risk AI systems** across **six sectors**, education among them; effective **2026-08-15** *(most sources; one does not state it)* |

| which systems | deadline |
|---|---|
| 🟢 existing systems in **healthcare, EDUCATION and finance**, live before **2026-08-15** | **2027-09-01** |
| existing systems in **all other sectors** | **2027-03-01** |
| systems put into operation **within six months after** Decision 33 takes effect | **2027-03-01** |
| during the transition | existing systems may keep running **unless a competent state agency finds serious risk** and orders suspension |

### 🟢 Settled, and two of this file's three green claims were wrong

- 🟢 **Line ~57's `2027-09` is CORRECT** — it is education's date, and it is the one cell that had it right.
- 🔴 **Opportunity 5's `1 Mar 2027` is WRONG for education.** That is the **all-other-sectors** date.
  🔴 **A live opportunity row therefore prices an education engagement against a deadline six months
  earlier than the one that governs it** — the error runs toward *urgency*, which is the direction a
  client notices.
- 🔴 **Line ~1733's "six-month grace period" is WRONG for education.** `2026-08-15` → `2027-09-01` is
  **~12.5 months**. The six-month figure is the *"within six months after Decision 33 takes effect"*
  clause, which is a different provision about **new** systems.

🟡 **THE BOUND, and it is `p388`'s request left UNMET.** 🔴 **No primary text was read.** Every policy
host this session can name answers `ENOTFOUND` through the proxy — `tilleke.com`,
`vietnam-briefing.com`, `aipolicydesk.com` all refused, as `moe.gov.cn`, `beijing.gov.cn` and
`cbseacademic.nic.in` did at `p400`. 🟢 So this is settled on **five-source concurrence plus a
mechanism that explains the disagreement**, which is strictly better than the contradiction it
replaces and strictly weaker than a read. 🔵 `p388`'s instruction stands for the next pass with
egress: the reads that would close it are the Decision 33 text and Decree 142 in the Vietnamese
official gazette.

### 🔵 What the other three legs returned: nothing unshelved

| region | leg returned | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **North America** | Ohio's July-1-2026 district-policy mandate · California **AB 1159** · Alabama **HB 329** · Maryland **SB 720** · Oregon **SB 1546** · DC's grading/discipline/IEP prohibition · 134 bills in 31 states · NYC's Sept-2026 student-facing moratorium · the Dear Colleague letter | 🔴 **all carried** |
| **EMEA** | AI Act enforcement from **2026-08-02** · Digital Omnibus in force **2026-07-27** · education as Annex III high-risk · the €2.64B/31.9% market figure · *"only 10 % of 450+ institutions have formal AI guidelines"* | 🔴 **all carried.** 🟡 One date conflict the leg raised — standalone Annex III at **2027-12-02** and embedded at **2028-08-02** against *"high-risk lands August 2026"* — is `p385`'s Art. 50 transitional distinction, already settled on this file |
| **LATAM** | **ILIA 2026**: 11 of 20 countries now carry AI content in school programmes, against **2 in 2024**; Chile first at **72.5** preparedness · Brazil **PL 2338/2023** passed the Senate Dec 2024, still pending in the Chamber · 92 % of students / 79 % of teachers using AI (Digital Education Council / Tec de Monterrey) · 85 % of countries with a national AI strategy, **only Brazil and Colombia funding it** | 🔴 **all carried** |

🟢 **So the regional-leg saturation `p405` measured is real and now has a fourth datapoint — with one
exception that was worth all four legs**, because it did not add a fact, it **repaired** one.

## Opportunities by region — `p406`–`p414`, SUPERSEDED by `p415`'s block above

### North America

🆕 `p414` 🔴 **Nothing new, and the gap is Canada and Mexico.** The North America leg returned only
US state K-12 instruments this base already holds (Idaho `SB 1227`, Ohio `HB 96`, Arizona `HB 4040`,
California `AB 1159`, federal `H.R. 8747`), plus *Claude for Teachers* free to verified US K-12
educators through **2027-06-30**. 🔵 **Declared gap, stated so silence does not read as coverage:**
the leg returned **no** federal education-AI instrument for **Canada** or **Mexico**, so the
opportunity rows above remain US-only in their evidence base — a North America engagement outside
the US is not yet served by this shelf.


🟢 **Sell the district AI-use policy as a deliverable, not as advice.** Ohio requires **every** K-12
district to adopt a formal AI policy by **2026-07-01** — 🆕 `p407` names the **instrument**, per
`P955`: **Ohio Revised Code § 3301.24**, added by **House Bill 96** (the 2025 budget bill), with the
state's own model policy due **2025-12-31** and published early **2026-01**. 🔴 **The statute
requires a policy to EXIST; it does not mandate teaching AI or using it** — so this is a
`district_ai_policy` duty, never a teaching mandate, and the policy-matrix row says so. 🔵 **The
deadline has now PASSED** (today is 2026-10-23), which changes the sale from "help us comply" to
"your policy exists and nobody has measured against it" — and 33–35 states plus Puerto Rico publish
guidance with no implementation capacity attached. 🟡 The constraint is procurement: the federal Dear
Colleague letter pushes **outcomes-based contracting**, and named Arkansas, Indiana, Louisiana,
Michigan and Texas for it. 🔵 So the saleable asset is a **policy-plus-evidence pack** — the policy
document, plus the measurement design that lets a district show the outcome it contracted for.


**🆕 `p409` · 2026-10-23 — this pass's addition to North America.**
🔴 **The regulatory shape is the opportunity.** Oklahoma and Maryland bar AI from high-stakes student
decisions and require human oversight; California AB 1159 bars student data from training. 🟢 **What
sells into that is an audit trail, not a model**: read-only, `GET`-only integrations against the
LMS/SIS of record, with every AI action logged against a named human approver. 🔵 The
`brightspace-mcp-server` family (MIT, shipping four times a day) and `blend-ed/openedx-mcp` are the
two LMS footholds; the **binding constraint is that the distinctive read-only D2L server
(`n35da`) is ungranted**, so a US engagement must either use the browser-client family or write its
own read-only server. 🟡 NYC's grade-8 moratorium is being copied — assume student-facing K-8 is
closed and sell **teacher-facing** workflow instead.


**🆕 `p410` · 2026-10-23 — this pass's addition to North America.**
🟢 **One refinement, and it widens the buyer.** **Arizona HB 4040** would require student-AI-use
policies of K-12 districts **and public universities** — most state instruments on these shelves stop
at K-12, so this is the rarer shape: a single statute that makes the **same** policy-and-audit
deliverable sellable to two different procurement offices. 🔴 **Still a bill**, so sell readiness and
say which it is. 🔵 Nothing else in this leg was unshelved: Maryland **SB 720** (17 mentions on these
shelves), Idaho **SB 1227** (11) and the ~100-bill session count are all carried. 🟡 The durable
number remains the policy gap — **86 % of education organisations using gen-AI, most without a
policy** — which is the governance engagement, not a tooling one.


**🆕 `p413` · 2026-10-23 — this pass's addition to North America.**
🟢 **A licence reading opens an engagement shape this base had been implicitly closing.**
[`openedx/edx-platform`](https://github.com/openedx/edx-platform)'s `master/LICENSE` (`35 136` B,
sha256 `e12d78569742`, read **2026-10-23**) appends an **EdX Inc. clarification** that a public,
independently available web service communicating with the platform **over RPC is not a work based
on it**, and that **"Corresponding Source" excludes** source for programs used solely to provide
such services. 🟢 **So the AI service beside Open edX is outside AGPL §13's network copyleft — the
copyleft exposure is in modifying `edx-platform`, not in building next to it.** 🔵 That is
`Pattern AM`, and it matters most here because the read-only, audit-trail architecture `p409`
prescribed for Oklahoma, Maryland and California AB 1159 is **exactly** a side-car. 🟡 The
clarification is EdX Inc.'s appended statement of intent, not an AGPL term — cite the payload, the
ref and the date, and take counsel per deployment.
🔴 **One procurement trap, measured.** [`academic-innovation/django-lti`](https://github.com/academic-innovation/django-lti)
(the MIT LTI 1.3 library this shape needs) declares its licence on PyPI **only in its OSI
classifier** — no `license` field, no `license_expression`. 🔴 A scan that reads one field reports it
**unlicensed** and a procurement office stops there, so put the classifier read in the licence
register before review, not after.

### EMEA

🆕 `p414` 🟡 **France states a preference for libre in its own policy language — the clearest demand
signal on this shelf, and it cannot be verified here.** The *cadre d'usage de l'IA en éducation*
instructs readers to *"privilégiez les solutions libres qui permettent un contrôle des corpus
utilisés ainsi que des procédures de traitement"*, set beside a **feuille de route IA 2026–2027**,
**La Forge des communs numériques éducatifs** as the state channel for libre educational resources,
a **Pix IA** pathway compulsory per the *Bulletin officiel* of **2026-02-05**, and an AI tutor in
**pilot classes from Q3 2026** with a **2027** review. 🟢 **The sale this implies is not "consider
open source" but "you have already mandated it — here is the integration capacity"**, and the
sovereignty-of-corpus justification means the argument is about control, not licence cost. 🔴 **Band
`reported`, search-channel only:** `education.gouv.fr`, `eduscol.education.fr`,
`forge.apps.education.fr`, `conseil-ia-numerique.fr` and `pix.fr` are **all five** `TUNNEL-REFUSED`
on this environment. 🔴 **A €230 M/3-year figure the leg offered is NOT carried** — one general-news
source, no official confirmation. 🔵 **Declared gap:** nothing on Germany's Länder rules or the AI
Act applied to education; `p413`'s German leg remains the last reading here. 🔴 And the expensive
part is that **La Forge is a SUPPLY host**: whatever libre education assets it carries cannot be
enumerated or licence-read from this environment at all.


🟢 **Annex III conformity for education, as a reusable asset.** Education sits in the AI Act's
high-risk tier for admission, evaluation and exam scoring; 🔴 **but enforcement has NOT begun** —
the Digital Omnibus (in force **2026-07-27**) defers the **Annex III** education obligations to
**2027-12-02**, and **2026-08-02** is the **Article 50** transparency date only, never the high-risk
one. 🟢 So the honest pitch is **readiness on a ~14-month clock**, not compliance against a date that
has passed; only **10 %** of 450+ surveyed institutions have formal guidelines. 🟢 **And the asset is PORTABLE** — the
same conformity work adapts to Vietnam's risk model and to Mexico's far more cheaply than it
rebuilds. 🔵 One asset, three markets.


**🆕 `p409` · 2026-10-23 — this pass's addition to EMEA.**
🔴 **2027-12-02 is the date to sell against.** Annex III stand-alone obligations bite education
scoring and admissions ranking then, and institutions that buy in 2026–27 will be asked for
conformity evidence they do not have. 🟢 **The offer is a conformity-ready assessment pipeline**:
documented data lineage, human-in-the-loop on every scoring decision, and the model slot pinned to a
named provider. 🔵 `OpenMAIC`'s central model governance (`openmaic.yml` — every AI use a named slot,
user-supplied keys forbiddable) is the one shelf asset that maps directly onto an AI Act technical
file. 🟡 Germany's €20 bn commitment and the UK's principles-based divergence mean **two different
sales motions inside one region** — do not quote a single EMEA playbook.


**🆕 `p410` · 2026-10-23 — this pass's addition to EMEA.**
🔴 **Nothing unshelved, and that is now worth money rather than worth noting.** The EMEA leg returned
the **Digital Omnibus, Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** deferral of Annex III education obligations to
**2027-12-02**, Article 50 transparency unchanged from **2026-08-02**, the education emotion-inference
ban live since **2025-02-02**, and the Article 4 AI-literacy duty in force. 🟢 **This base already
carries every one** — the Omnibus at 21 mentions, `2027-12-02` at 13. 🔵 **This is the third
independent channel to confirm the same timeline, so the EMEA regulatory layer is SETTLED: a
successor should stop re-buying it and spend the leg elsewhere.** 🟡 The sellable consequence is
unchanged and sharpened by repetition: the deferral moved the **deadline**, not the **work** —
Article 50 and Article 4 bite now, and an institution that waits for December 2027 has two live
duties and no evidence for either.


**🆕 `p413` · 2026-10-23 — this pass's addition to EMEA.**
🟢 **A ministry has become a supplier, and the asset is reusable.** **KIMADU** — Universität Siegen
with NRW's Ministerium für Schule und Bildung, **above €1 M** of state funding, **2025-02-01 →
2027-07-31**, **25** schools — publishes **eleven** didactic German-subject agents whose system
prompts are **OER under `CC BY-SA 4.0`**, explicitly downloadable, adaptable and transferable into
one's own environment. 🟢 **That is pedagogy a client does not have to pay to invent, already
validated in a state programme**, and it is the first permissively-licensed public-sector agent
supply this base has shelved in EMEA.
🔴 **Two boundaries decide whether it is sellable.** First, the grant is **scoped**: the `KIM`
document limits it to *"ausschließlich den Prompt und unsere Informationen zur Nutzung"* — the
**three knowledge-base libraries** the agent runs with sit **outside** it. 🟢 That is the engagement:
the ministry supplies the prompt, the client supplies the corpus, Globant supplies the pinning and
retrieval. Second, `CC BY-SA 4.0` is **share-alike**, so client-specific pedagogy belongs in the
supplied libraries, never in a forked prompt. 🔵 `Pattern AN` is the recipe.
🔴 **And a standing limit on EMEA diligence, now measured rather than assumed.** Every German,
Portuguese and EU institutional host this pass probed answered `http=000` — connection never
established — against control hosts that returned `200` in the same loop. 🟡 **EMEA statute and
institutional facts are search-channel on this environment and will stay so**, so an EMEA proposal
should cite the relayed source openly and price first-hand legal verification as client-side work.

### APAC

🆕 `p414` 🟡 **Japan is building education AI itself, and it has neither a licence nor a repository
yet — which is the moment to be in the room.** **MEXT** is to develop its **own** education-specific
AI application, aiming at **FY2027** demonstration research, with development and school
information-security costs placed in the **FY2027 概算要求** — 🔴 **a budget request, not an
appropriation**, so the programme is not yet funded. 🔴 **No licence and no repository are stated
anywhere in the leg.** 🟢 Read beside `p413`'s **KIMADU** (Germany: `CC BY-SA 4.0` prompts, **no**
repository), the shape now has two rungs — *grant without address*, and *neither grant nor address* —
and **Japan is the earlier one**. 🔵 **The opportunity is the grant decision itself**: a ministry that
has not chosen a licence can still be advised toward one, and the procurement question a studio will
face in **FY2027** is *"can you take over what the ministry built"* — answerable only if the grant
permits it. 🟡 **Search-channel only**: `mext.go.jp` and `asahi.com` are both `TUNNEL-REFUSED`. 🔵
Everything else the leg returned is carried (Korea's `AI Basic Act`, Vietnam's `Decision 33/2026`,
China's age-six mandate); `KRW 30 M` and a `$960 M` Korean plan are single-relay and **not** carried.


🟢 **The clearest dated roadmap on earth, and almost nobody is selling against it — but price it to
the RIGHT date.** Education is one of six high-risk sectors under Decision 33, and an education
system **already live before 2026-08-15 has until 2027-09-01**, not 2027-03-01. 🟡 **Two consequences
that run opposite ways.** The deadline is **~11 months out**, not five, so an urgency pitch keyed on
March is both wrong and checkable by the client's own counsel. 🟢 But the grace period **rewards
being already deployed**: a system live before the cutoff buys 12.5 months, and one deployed after it
buys six. 🔵 **That makes "deploy before the cutoff" a real, dated procurement argument** — and the
cutoff has already passed, so for any *new* engagement the six-month clause is the one that governs.
🔵 Korea's `AI Basic Act` (in force **2026-01-22**) remains the regime a Korean engagement is actually
governed by. 🆕 **`p407` ran the Korean leg IN LANGUAGE** (invariant `I4`) and reports an
**informed nil at ministry level**: no Korean **Ministry of Education** 2026 AI instrument and no
open-source guidance surfaced. 🟢 What it did return is **provincial and budgeted** — the
**Daejeon** Metropolitan Office of Education's 2026 policy funds **six** AI/digital tool
subscriptions at **≈₩300 million**, with teacher research groups building lesson-design and
formative-assessment use. 🔴 **Whether those six are open source is NOT established** and the
source does not say. 🔵 **This is Mexico's and Canada's shape a third time** (`P955`): in a
devolved system the instrument is provincial, and a national query reports an empty world.

🆕 **`p408` — the India opportunity is a MAINTENANCE sale, and the grant is already won.**
🟢 **Sunbird ED is MIT** — the building block **DIKSHA** runs on — which is the licence outcome
[`repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md) opens by saying education never gets. 🔴 **And the
published implementation is stale: the portal `311 d` since HEAD, the mobile app `399 d`, and
`Sunbird-Ed/sunbird-learning-platform` resolves no ref at all.** 🟡 **That inverts the usual pitch.**
Against Moodle or Open edX a studio sells *around* the grant; against Sunbird there is nothing to
sell around, so the engagement is **fork, pin, CVE-sweep and maintain**, and the money sits in the
staffing plan rather than in legal review. 🟢 It prices cleanly because it is checkable: every
number above is a `git` read a client can repeat without credentials. 🔴 **The mandatory step 0 is
provenance, not licensing:** `p408` found a Sunbird documentation fork carrying **AGPL-3.0**,
returned *above* upstream in search, holding a **byte-identical** copy of the grant upstream
abandoned on **2018-06-21**. A badge-read confirms that AGPL and is right to. 🔵 Recipe and the
four-command check: `Pattern AG` in [`compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md).


**🆕 `p409` · 2026-10-23 — this pass's addition to APAC.**
🔴 **Vietnam Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg names automated assessment and behavioural monitoring as
high-risk, which is precisely what an education AI pilot does.** 🟢 The opportunity is to arrive with
the risk classification already answered. 🔵 India remains the one market where the **whole stack can
be MIT** — see `compose/patterns.md` Pattern AG and `repos/foundations.md` on the DPI layer going
stale (311/399 days), which is itself the opening: the national reference implementations are
permissive and under-maintained, so a studio can contribute upstream and own the integration.
🟡 Korea's grace period ends **2027-01-22**; work booked in 2026 should be written to the Framework
Act as if penalties already applied.


**🆕 `p410` · 2026-10-23 — this pass's addition to APAC, and it is where this pass's legs actually
paid.**
🟢 **Four facts these shelves did not carry, all band `reported`.** **Singapore** is the clearest
engagement: a **National AI Impact Programme** training **40,000 technology professionals over three
years**, baseline AI training mandated for **all higher-education students from 2027**, and **AI
Verify** functioning as a de-facto gate for vendors selling into regulated sectors. 🔵 Read together
those are one shape — **a workforce-scale curriculum mandate with a vendor-assurance regime attached**
— which is a capability-building and conformance engagement, not a tutor deployment. 🟢 **India**
pairs a mandate with money: AI and computational thinking compulsory **from Class 3 in 2026–27**,
behind a **₹500 crore** centre of excellence and the **₹10,372 crore** IndiaAI mission; the shelves
held the mandate, not the funding. 🟡 **Beijing** adds a measurable floor — **≥8 hours** of AI
instruction per student per year — which is the kind of number a procurement document can be written
against. 🔴 **All four are secondary-source**, and the compulsory-curriculum framing is contested
inside this very leg, so none supersedes `p406`'s statute-level detail: cite them as `reported`.


**🆕 `p413` · 2026-10-23 — this pass's addition to APAC.**
🔴 **Nil on facts, and stated as nil: the English APAC leg returned nothing unshelved for a fifth
consecutive pass.** Vietnam's **2026-03-01** law with education among six high-risk sectors, Korea's
**AI Basic Act**, China's mandatory registration frameworks, Singapore's and Japan's voluntary
toolkits, Australia and New Zealand's light-touch posture and the **Ipsos Education Monitor 2026**
ban-support split are **all carried**. 🔵 No in-language APAC leg was run this pass; that is the
unspent budget a successor should take first, because Spanish, Portuguese and German have each
broken saturation while English has not.
🟢 **What does carry into APAC is the architecture, not a new fact.** `Pattern AM`'s Open edX RPC
side-car is **licence-portable**: the EdX Inc. clarification sits in the payload, not in a
jurisdiction, so the same side-car design that answers EMEA's Annex III readiness answers a
high-risk classification under Vietnam's regime — **one architecture, three regulatory stories**,
which is the same portability argument the EMEA block already makes for conformity work.

### LATAM

🆕 `p414` 🔴 **Zero unshelved, and the structural gap is now worth naming as a gap rather than a
finding.** The Spanish leg returned `Estado de México`'s April 2026 education-law reform — carried
here **with** its *media superior* / *superior* scope — plus `PL 2338/2023`, Perú and El Salvador,
all carried. 🔵 The only new token is the article number, **`artículo 61`**, which is a label on a
carried fact. 🔴 A *73 % of Mexican undergraduates* figure does **not** enter: low-traceability blog,
single source. 🔴 **Declared gap, and it is the region's real asymmetry: no LATAM leg across six
passes has returned a state-published education-AI REPOSITORY.** EMEA produces assessment and
compliance layers, APAC produces policy and now state builds; LATAM's presence on these shelves is
**policy-only on the supply axis**. 🟢 For a studio that is an opening, not a deficit — the region's
open-source education supply is being built by nobody in particular, so a LATAM engagement starts
from the global permissive shelf plus local integration, and the integration is the billable part.


🟢 **The curriculum wave arrived and the capacity did not.** ILIA 2026: **11 of 20** countries now
carry AI content in school programmes against **2 in 2024** — a 5.5× expansion in two years — while
**only Brazil and Colombia** fund their national AI strategy, and universities operate in what the
region's own literature calls a *vacío normativo*. 🟡 With **92 % of students and 79 % of teachers**
already using AI, the gap is not adoption, it is **governance and teacher capability**. 🔵 And the
regulatory asset is the same one EMEA pays for: Brazil's **PL 2338/2023** is risk-tiered on the EU
model, so an Annex III pack ports. 🔴 It is still pending in the Chamber of Deputies — so sell
**readiness**, not compliance, and say which it is.


**🆕 `p409` · 2026-10-23 — this pass's addition to LATAM.**
🔴 **UNESCO IESALC's finding — adoption outpacing governance across 200 institutions in 19
countries — is the engagement, stated by the region's own research body.** 🟢 With **92 % of students
and 79 % of faculty already using AI** but **88 % of faculty only minimally-to-moderately engaged**
and assessment the least-common use, the gap is not adoption and not enthusiasm (72 % positive, vs
57 % globally) — it is **institutional policy and assessment integrity**. Sell the governance layer:
an institutional AI-use policy, an assessment-redesign programme, and the audit trail to evidence
both. 🔴 **Do not plan on LATAM-native agent supply**: `Gap 386-C` holds for a further pass, and the
two addresses an in-language leg returned this pass are both **ungranted**, so neither can be built
on. 🔵 Build on the Global shelf and localise.


**🆕 `p410` · 2026-10-23 — this pass's addition to LATAM, and it CORRECTS `p409`'s copy above.**
🔴 **`p409` wrote, in this block: *“Do not plan on LATAM-native agent supply … the two addresses an
in-language leg returned this pass are both ungranted, so neither can be built on.”* That is now too
strong.** 🟢 A leg phrased **in Portuguese** returned
[`luisricar-do/portugol-ai-tutor`](https://github.com/luisricar-do/portugol-ai-tutor) — **live by 2
days** (HEAD `84ae17c` 2026-10-21), a **Socratic** Portugol tutor out of a **UNIFEI** master's
dissertation, and **granted**: GPL-3.0, payload 35,141 B. 🔵 **The advice becomes conditional rather
than negative, and the two are different proposals:** GPL-3.0 is **not** AGPL, so hosting it as a
service is not distribution and is fine — embedding modified code in a proprietary client deliverable
is not. 🟡 **The deeper correction is methodological:** `p409`'s *“not one Portuguese-language
project”* was a property of its **leg's language**, which is the same error `p387` recorded when it
closed `Gap 386-C`. **Run the leg in the region's language before writing a supply claim about the
region.** 🔵 Two policy facts added, both band `reported` and both narrow: **Estado de México**
reformed its education law in **April 2026** for graduated AI use in *media superior* and *superior*
(sub-national — it does not generalise to Mexico), and **Colombia** has **PL 043/2025 Senado ·
324/2025 Cámara** in progress alongside `Ley 2626 de 2026`, which these shelves already hold at a
firmer band. 🔴 Unchanged and still the engagement: **92 % of students and 79 % of faculty using AI
against 19 % of institutions with an approved internal policy.**


**🆕 `p413` · 2026-10-23 — this pass's addition to LATAM.**
🟢 **The curriculum-grounding instrument for Brazil is permissive, maintained and splits code from
data — which is exactly the shape a client engagement needs.** [`bncc-dev/bncc-pacotes`](https://github.com/bncc-dev/bncc-pacotes)
ships as `bncc` **`0.5.1`** on PyPI (**7** releases, last upload **2026-10-15**), declaring **`MIT`**
in PEP 639's `license_expression`, and its metadata names **four** repository URLs — including
[`bncc-dev/bncc-dados`](https://github.com/bncc-dev/bncc-dados) as a **separate data repository**.
🟢 **So the BNCC curriculum can be grounded under MIT for the code and versioned separately for the
data**, which is step 6 of `Pattern AM` and the cheapest regional differentiator on this shelf.
🔴 **The regulatory half is moving and this base cannot yet verify it.** Brazil's **CNE** approved a
proposed *marco regulatório* for AI in education with public consultation to **17 June**; its
current status is **unconfirmed here**, and MEC's **AVAMEC** teacher course and the **Toolzz**
no-code agent platform are likewise search-channel only. 🔴 Every Lusophone authority host probed —
`diariodarepublica.pt` among them — answered `http=000`. 🟡 **So sell against the demand signal, not
against a statute**: the teacher-training and curriculum-grounding work is fundable today, while the
compliance deliverable waits on a framework whose text this base cannot read.

### Global

🆕 `p414` 🔴 **One cross-region constraint hardened this pass, and it changes what a licence review
can promise.** An in-tree `LICENSE` is now **two** questions, not one: *which text is this* — the
AGPL-3.0 lattice answers it in-tree, four byte-distinct presentations of one licence — and *is the
text even here*, where `GarethManning/education-agent-skills` answers **no** on **both** its
subtrees, with the operative CC BY-SA 4.0 legalcode on a host that is `TUNNEL-REFUSED` from this
environment. 🟢 **The commercial consequence is concrete:** a deliverable's licence review can be
completed in-tree for source platforms (Open edX, Moodle, the AGPL verticals) and **cannot** be
completed in-tree for the skill-packs and prompt assets that are this industry's newest supply. 🔵
So a studio pricing a compliance review should price those two classes differently, and should say
which of the two a component falls into before quoting it.


🟢 **The cross-region asset is the conformity pack, and it is the one thing that compounds.** The
same Annex III work prices against the EU's high-risk tier, Vietnam's Decision 33 risk model and
Brazil's `PL 2338/2023` — all three are risk-tiered, so the artefact ports rather than rebuilds.
🟡 **And the cross-region constraint is licence sorting, not technology:** `T84` — ECL-2.0 now sits
under **two** platforms a client may already run (Sakai, Opencast), and a `"MIT / Apache 2.0 / BSD
only"` filter drops both toward **over**-caution. 🔵 Globally applicable, so recorded here rather
than four times.

🟢 **The build-vs-compose rule, from this pass's own measurement (`T83`):** the institutional
platform tier decayed **0 %** over 104 addresses and the agent tier **5.0 %** over 238. Put the
**integration contract** at the centre of a design, not the agent — in every region.

---


**🆕 `p410` · 2026-10-23 — this pass's addition to Global.**
🔴 **A due-diligence finding that prices into every region's SOW.** Of eight addresses read
first-hand this pass, **three** carry a grant path whose content does not support the grant its
metadata claims — an 874 B *“Apache License”* notice (**7.7 %** of Apache-2.0's 11,358 B) whose
manifest says `MIT`; a dual grant whose **AGPL half is 302 B** (**0.9 %** of an AGPL payload) while
its manifest names the permissive half only; and a fork whose manifest carries **no `license` field
at all**. 🔵 **The trend is not more unlicensed code — it is more *plausibly* licensed code**, and
plausibility is exactly what an automated check reads. 🟢 **Sellable as a service, in any region:** a
grant-resolution pass over a client's existing open-source estate, delivered as a per-address verdict
with its channels (`Pattern AI`). 🔴 **And one operational term belongs in every contract now:**
npm `brightspace-mcp-server` moved **5.0.8 → 5.0.13 inside a single day**, read twice on the same
date with two answers — so **pin exact versions and never write `@latest` into a runbook.** 🟡 The
scarcity has also shifted: the two addresses that moved gaps this pass are both essentially
**single-maintainer** projects, so the diligence question is no longer *“is there a grant?”* but
**“who answers when it breaks?”** — and that question has no SPDX identifier.
